,{\"_92\":253,\"_238\":43,\"_239\":43,\"_240\":-5},\"36\",{\"_92\":252,\"_238\":43,\"_239\":43,\"_240\":-5},\"37\",{\"_92\":251,\"_238\":43,\"_239\":43,\"_240\":-5},\"38\",{\"_92\":250,\"_238\":43,\"_239\":43,\"_240\":-5},\"39\",{\"_92\":249,\"_238\":43,\"_239\":43,\"_240\":-5},\"40\",{\"_92\":248,\"_238\":43,\"_239\":43,\"_240\":-5},\"41\",{\"_92\":247,\"_238\":43,\"_239\":43,\"_240\":-5},\"42\",{\"_92\":246,\"_238\":43,\"_239\":43,\"_240\":-5},\"43\",{\"_92\":245,\"_238\":43,\"_239\":43,\"_240\":-5},\"44\",{\"_92\":244,\"_238\":43,\"_239\":43,\"_240\":-5},\"45\",{\"_92\":243,\"_238\":43,\"_239\":43,\"_240\":-5},\"46\",{\"_92\":242,\"_238\":43,\"_239\":43,\"_240\":-5},\"47\",{\"_92\":241,\"_238\":43,\"_239\":43,\"_240\":-5},\"48\",{\"_92\":237,\"_238\":43,\"_239\":43,\"_240\":-5},\"c8c6t2\",\"edited\",\"previewable\",\"preview_language\",\"l4srwx\",\"xkqrgs\",\"v9gzsl\",\"kltwf6\",\"yoku2c\",\"hcjrzd\",\"j6xxhi\",\"lzplmv\",\"vfyx7k\",\"4zxgge\",\"yz8284\",\"qntapu\",\"9m48q5\",\"i2v61f\",\"quvo7o\",\"sffdtf\",\"28qwxb\",\"yo81hm\",\"mlmi4a\",\"4jy1wd\",\"gtj8tm\",\"daq826\",\"p5oghz\",\"pdk6a5\",\"gak9fy\",\"p0ozrt\",\"opxyzg\",\"p620df\",\"76jsue\",\"90dmip\",\"0cwnm8\",\"7nyex2\",\"klbyz5\",\"64wwg5\",\"76i0w9\",\"3nm4ys\",\"lsnpx9\",\"3duybm\",\"yns4cm\",\"a49he8\",\"go8qf8\",\"ryopqc\",\"p5n60b\",\"isum6d\",\"otvykh\",\"bgqqik\",\"phki93\",\"q02n4q\",\"type\",\"stop\",\"stop_tokens\",[293],200002,\"content_type\",\"text\",\"parts\",[298],\"```markdown\\n# Stellantis Market Impact Intelligence — Master Build Prompt\\n\\nYou are a senior software architect, data engineer, quantitative analyst, ML engineer, AI engineer, and cloud engineer.\\n\\nBuild a complete, modular, zero-cost/open-source project called:\\n\\n# Stellantis Market Impact Intelligence\\n\\n## 1. PROJECT OBJECTIVE\\n\\nBuild an AI-powered financial and corporate intelligence platform centered on Stellantis (STLA).\\n\\nThe core question the system should answer is:\\n\\n\u003e \\\"When something materially affects Stellantis, which other publicly traded companies may be affected, how have they historically reacted, what business relationship connects them, and what evidence explains the relationship?\\\"\\n\\nThe system must go substantially beyond simple stock correlation.\\n\\nIt should combine:\\n\\n- Financial market data\\n- Event detection\\n- Statistical analysis\\n- Event studies\\n- News intelligence\\n- Company filings\\n- Corporate relationship mapping\\n- Supply-chain analysis\\n- Knowledge graphs\\n- Vector search\\n- RAG\\n- Local LLMs\\n- Autonomous investigation\\n- API development\\n- Dashboarding\\n- Docker\\n- Kubernetes\\n- Infrastructure as Code\\n- Observability\\n- CI/CD\\n\\nThe final system should function as a market-intelligence and corporate-dependency analysis platform, NOT as a trading or investment recommendation system.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 2. IMPORTANT CONSTRAINTS\\n\\nThe entire project should be developable at ₹0.\\n\\nPrefer:\\n\\n- Free/public data sources\\n- Open-source software\\n- Local computation\\n- Local databases\\n- Local LLMs\\n- Free GitHub tooling\\n- Local Docker/Kubernetes\\n\\nDo NOT depend on paid APIs.\\n\\nDo NOT require cloud infrastructure during development.\\n\\nCloud deployment should be optional.\\n\\nDo NOT build investment recommendations.\\n\\nDo NOT predict stock prices.\\n\\nDo NOT claim that correlation proves causation.\\n\\nClearly distinguish:\\n\\n1. Observed market behavior\\n2. Statistical relationships\\n3. Documented business relationships\\n4. AI-generated hypotheses\\n\\nNever invent company relationships, news, statistics, or evidence.\\n\\nEvery factual relationship should have supporting evidence where possible.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 3. TARGET ARCHITECTURE\\n\\nBuild the system toward this architecture:\\n\\n```text\\n                         DATA SOURCES\\n                              │\\n             ┌────────────────┼────────────────┐\\n             │                │                │\\n             ▼                ▼                ▼\\n        Market Data         News          Company Filings\\n             │                │                │\\n             └────────────────┼────────────────┘\\n                              ▼\\n                       DATA INGESTION\\n                              │\\n                              ▼\\n                    PARQUET / DATA LAKE\\n                              │\\n                              ▼\\n                         DUCKDB\\n                              │\\n              ┌───────────────┼────────────────┐\\n              ▼               ▼                ▼\\n       EVENT ENGINE      ANALYTICS         NLP ENGINE\\n              │               │                │\\n              └───────────────┼────────────────┘\\n                              ▼\\n                    IMPACT ANALYSIS ENGINE\\n                              │\\n              ┌───────────────┼────────────────┐\\n              ▼               ▼                ▼\\n        Correlation       Event Study     Anomaly Detection\\n              │               │                │\\n              └───────────────┼────────────────┘\\n                              ▼\\n                    CORPORATE KNOWLEDGE GRAPH\\n                              │\\n                              ▼\\n                       VECTOR DATABASE\\n                              │\\n                              ▼\\n                         LOCAL RAG\\n                              │\\n                              ▼\\n                      LOCAL LLM / AGENT\\n                              │\\n                              ▼\\n                           FASTAPI\\n                              │\\n                              ▼\\n                         DASHBOARD\\n                              │\\n              ───────── INFRASTRUCTURE ─────────\\n                              │\\n            Docker → Kubernetes → Terraform\\n                              │\\n        OpenTelemetry → Prometheus → Grafana\\n                              │\\n                  GitHub Actions / CI-CD\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 4. TECHNOLOGY STACK\\n\\nUse the following stack unless there is a strong technical reason to change something:\\n\\n## Programming\\n\\n- Python\\n- SQL\\n- Bash\\n\\n## Data\\n\\n- Pandas and/or Polars\\n- PyArrow\\n- DuckDB\\n- Parquet\\n\\n## Financial Analysis\\n\\n- NumPy\\n- SciPy\\n- Statsmodels\\n- Scikit-learn\\n\\n## Market Data\\n\\nPrefer free/public sources such as:\\n\\n- yfinance\\n- Stooq\\n- other legally accessible public sources\\n\\nDo not rely on paid financial APIs.\\n\\n## News\\n\\nUse:\\n\\n- RSS\\n- publicly accessible news sources\\n- company press releases\\n- public announcements\\n\\nRespect source terms and rate limits.\\n\\n## Corporate Documents\\n\\nUse publicly available:\\n\\n- SEC filings\\n- company annual reports\\n- investor presentations\\n- earnings materials\\n- company announcements\\n\\n## Knowledge Graph\\n\\nStart with:\\n\\n- NetworkX\\n\\nOptionally support:\\n\\n- Neo4j Community Edition locally\\n\\n## Vector Database\\n\\nUse either:\\n\\n- FAISS\\n- Chroma\\n\\n## Embeddings\\n\\nUse:\\n\\n- Sentence Transformers\\n- Hugging Face models\\n\\n## LLM\\n\\nUse:\\n\\n- Ollama\\n- local open-source models\\n\\nThe AI layer must not require OpenAI API usage.\\n\\n## Backend\\n\\n- FastAPI\\n- Pydantic\\n\\n## Dashboard\\n\\nStart with:\\n\\n- Streamlit\\n\\nKeep the architecture flexible enough to replace it with React later.\\n\\n## Infrastructure\\n\\n- Docker\\n- Docker Compose\\n- Kubernetes\\n- Terraform\\n\\n## Observability\\n\\n- OpenTelemetry\\n- Prometheus\\n- Grafana\\n- structured logging\\n\\n## CI/CD\\n\\n- GitHub Actions\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 5. REPOSITORY STRUCTURE\\n\\nCreate a professional repository:\\n\\n```text\\nstellantis-market-intelligence/\\n│\\n├── README.md\\n├── LICENSE\\n├── .gitignore\\n├── .env.example\\n├── requirements.txt\\n├── pyproject.toml\\n├── docker-compose.yml\\n├── Makefile\\n│\\n├── config/\\n│   ├── companies.yaml\\n│   ├── benchmarks.yaml\\n│   ├── event_rules.yaml\\n│   └── sources.yaml\\n│\\n├── data/\\n│   ├── raw/\\n│   ├── processed/\\n│   ├── features/\\n│   └── reports/\\n│\\n├── ingestion/\\n│   ├── market_data.py\\n│   ├── news.py\\n│   ├── filings.py\\n│   └── common.py\\n│\\n├── storage/\\n│   ├── duckdb.py\\n│   ├── parquet.py\\n│   └── schemas.py\\n│\\n├── analytics/\\n│   ├── returns.py\\n│   ├── volatility.py\\n│   ├── correlation.py\\n│   ├── beta.py\\n│   ├── event_study.py\\n│   ├── abnormal_returns.py\\n│   ├── anomaly_detection.py\\n│   ├── lead_lag.py\\n│   └── regime_detection.py\\n│\\n├── events/\\n│   ├── detector.py\\n│   ├── classifier.py\\n│   └── similarity.py\\n│\\n├── relationships/\\n│   ├── graph.py\\n│   ├── entities.py\\n│   ├── relationships.py\\n│   └── queries.py\\n│\\n├── ai/\\n│   ├── embeddings.py\\n│   ├── vector_store.py\\n│   ├── retrieval.py\\n│   ├── rag.py\\n│   ├── prompts.py\\n│   └── agent.py\\n│\\n├── api/\\n│   ├── main.py\\n│   ├── models.py\\n│   ├── routes/\\n│   └── services/\\n│\\n├── dashboard/\\n│   ├── app.py\\n│   ├── pages/\\n│   └── components/\\n│\\n├── reports/\\n│   ├── generator.py\\n│   └── templates/\\n│\\n├── infrastructure/\\n│   ├── docker/\\n│   ├── kubernetes/\\n│   ├── helm/\\n│   └── terraform/\\n│\\n├── observability/\\n│   ├── prometheus/\\n│   ├── grafana/\\n│   └── otel/\\n│\\n├── tests/\\n│   ├── unit/\\n│   ├── integration/\\n│   └── fixtures/\\n│\\n└── .github/\\n    └── workflows/\\n        ├── tests.yml\\n        └── build.yml\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 6. PHASE 1 — MARKET DATA FOUNDATION\\n\\nBuild the market-data ingestion layer.\\n\\nPrimary company:\\n\\n```text\\nStellantis\\nTicker: STLA\\n```\\n\\nCreate a configurable company universe.\\n\\nInitially include:\\n\\n- Stellantis\\n- Toyota\\n- Volkswagen\\n- Ford\\n- General Motors\\n- Tesla\\n- BMW\\n- Mercedes-Benz\\n- Renault\\n- Hyundai/Kia\\n- other relevant automotive companies\\n\\nDo not hardcode the system around these companies.\\n\\nThe company universe must be configurable.\\n\\nRetrieve:\\n\\n- Open\\n- High\\n- Low\\n- Close\\n- Adjusted Close\\n- Volume\\n\\nSupport:\\n\\n- ticker\\n- start date\\n- end date\\n- interval\\n\\nSave raw data as Parquet.\\n\\nCreate DuckDB tables:\\n\\n```text\\ncompanies\\nprice_data\\n```\\n\\nInclude:\\n\\n```text\\nticker\\ncompany_name\\nexchange\\nsector\\ndate\\nopen\\nhigh\\nlow\\nclose\\nadjusted_close\\nvolume\\n```\\n\\nImplement validation:\\n\\n- duplicate dates\\n- missing values\\n- invalid prices\\n- missing trading days\\n- API failures\\n- malformed records\\n\\nImplement structured logging.\\n\\nImplement:\\n\\n```bash\\npython -m ingestion.market_data --ticker STLA\\n```\\n\\nWrite unit tests.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 7. PHASE 2 — MARKET EVENT DETECTION\\n\\nBuild an event-detection engine.\\n\\nCalculate:\\n\\n- daily return\\n- log return\\n- rolling volatility\\n- rolling mean\\n- rolling standard deviation\\n- return z-score\\n- drawdown\\n- volume z-score\\n\\nSupport event detection using:\\n\\n1. Absolute return threshold\\n2. Volatility-adjusted return\\n3. Z-score\\n4. Volume anomaly\\n5. Combined event score\\n\\nEvent schema:\\n\\n```text\\nevent_id\\nticker\\nevent_date\\nreturn\\nz_score\\nvolume_z_score\\nvolatility\\nseverity\\ndirection\\ndetection_method\\n```\\n\\nStore events in DuckDB.\\n\\nImplement:\\n\\n```bash\\npython -m analytics.detect_events\\n```\\n\\nDo not determine the cause yet.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 8. PHASE 3 — CORRELATION \u0026 IMPACT ANALYSIS\\n\\nFor every company calculate relative to STLA:\\n\\n- Pearson correlation\\n- Spearman correlation\\n- rolling correlation\\n- beta\\n- R²\\n- p-value\\n- confidence interval\\n- sample size\\n\\nPerform event-window analysis:\\n\\n```text\\nT-5\\nT-3\\nT-1\\nT\\nT+1\\nT+3\\nT+5\\n```\\n\\nCalculate:\\n\\n- company return\\n- cumulative return\\n- abnormal return\\n- market-adjusted return\\n- sector-adjusted return\\n\\nCreate tables:\\n\\n```text\\ncorrelations\\nrolling_correlations\\nevent_returns\\nabnormal_returns\\n```\\n\\nDo not equate correlation with causation.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 9. PHASE 4 — MARKET VS SECTOR VS STELLANTIS-SPECIFIC\\n\\nAdd configurable benchmarks:\\n\\n- broad market index\\n- automotive sector benchmark\\n- relevant regional index\\n\\nFor each STLA event calculate:\\n\\n```text\\nSTLA return\\nmarket return\\nsector return\\nSTLA abnormal return\\nmarket-adjusted return\\nsector-adjusted return\\n```\\n\\nClassify events descriptively:\\n\\n```text\\nSTLA-specific\\nsector-wide\\nmarket-wide\\nmixed\\n```\\n\\nDo not make causal claims from this classification.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 10. PHASE 5 — NEWS INTELLIGENCE\\n\\nBuild a free news ingestion pipeline.\\n\\nUse:\\n\\n- RSS feeds\\n- company press releases\\n- public announcements\\n- legally accessible public sources\\n\\nStore:\\n\\n```text\\narticle_id\\ntimestamp\\ntitle\\nsource\\nurl\\nsummary/content where permitted\\nmentioned_companies\\nticker\\npublication_time\\n```\\n\\nImplement:\\n\\n- deduplication\\n- normalization\\n- source tracking\\n- timestamp normalization\\n\\nClassify news into:\\n\\n```text\\nEarnings\\nProduction\\nFactory\\nSupply Chain\\nPartnership\\nAcquisition\\nRegulation\\nRecall\\nProduct\\nEV Strategy\\nBattery\\nManagement\\nLabor\\nGeopolitical\\nMacro\\nOther\\n```\\n\\nInitially use deterministic/rule-based classification.\\n\\nFor each STLA event:\\n\\n1. Search the relevant time window.\\n2. Retrieve related articles.\\n3. Rank by relevance.\\n4. Identify possible catalysts.\\n\\nDo not claim an article caused the stock movement.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 11. PHASE 6 — CORPORATE KNOWLEDGE GRAPH\\n\\nBuild a corporate relationship graph.\\n\\nEntities:\\n\\n- companies\\n- Stellantis brands\\n- suppliers\\n- partners\\n- competitors\\n- technologies\\n- products\\n- manufacturing facilities\\n- geographic regions\\n\\nRelationship types:\\n\\n```text\\nsupplier\\ncustomer\\npartner\\ncompetitor\\ntechnology_partner\\njoint_venture\\nmanufacturing\\nlogistics\\nbattery_supplier\\nsemiconductor_supplier\\ncloud_provider\\nsoftware_partner\\n```\\n\\nEach relationship must contain:\\n\\n```text\\nsource\\nevidence_url\\ndate_discovered\\nrelationship_start\\nrelationship_end\\nconfidence\\ndescription\\n```\\n\\nNever invent relationships.\\n\\nEvery relationship should have evidence.\\n\\nSupport queries:\\n\\n```text\\nShow direct suppliers of Stellantis.\\n\\nShow companies within two relationship hops.\\n\\nShow battery-related relationships.\\n\\nShow semiconductor-related relationships.\\n\\nShow companies whose relationship with STLA changed over time.\\n```\\n\\nStart with NetworkX.\\n\\nKeep the abstraction compatible with Neo4j later.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 12. PHASE 7 — SUPPLY-CHAIN IMPACT PROPAGATION\\n\\nConnect the market analytics engine with the corporate graph.\\n\\nWhen STLA experiences a significant event:\\n\\n1. Detect event.\\n2. Classify event.\\n3. Identify affected business areas.\\n4. Traverse relationship graph.\\n5. Identify connected companies.\\n6. Retrieve historical stock behavior.\\n7. Calculate historical sensitivity.\\n8. Compare current reaction with historical behavior.\\n\\nClassify:\\n\\n```text\\nFIRST ORDER\\nDirect STLA relationship.\\n\\nSECOND ORDER\\nConnected through another company.\\n\\nTHIRD ORDER\\nIndirect ecosystem exposure.\\n```\\n\\nFor every company calculate:\\n\\n```text\\nrelationship_type\\ngraph_distance\\nhistorical_correlation\\nevent_sensitivity\\nreaction_frequency\\nevidence_strength\\n```\\n\\nThe output should answer:\\n\\n\u003e \\\"Which companies are worth investigating when this type of STLA event occurs?\\\"\\n\\nDo not make trading recommendations.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 13. PHASE 8 — ADVANCED EVENT STUDY\\n\\nBuild a rigorous event-study framework.\\n\\nFor each event:\\n\\nCalculate:\\n\\n- expected return\\n- actual return\\n- abnormal return\\n- cumulative abnormal return\\n- benchmark-adjusted return\\n\\nSupport event windows:\\n\\n```text\\n[-5,+5]\\n[-3,+3]\\n[-1,+1]\\n[0,+1]\\n[0,+3]\\n[0,+5]\\n```\\n\\nPerform statistical significance testing.\\n\\nReport:\\n\\n```text\\nevent\\ncompany\\nwindow\\nabnormal_return\\nCAR\\np_value\\nconfidence_interval\\nsample_size\\n```\\n\\nDo not overstate statistical significance.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 14. PHASE 9 — LEAD/LAG ANALYSIS\\n\\nDetermine whether companies historically move before or after STLA.\\n\\nTest:\\n\\n```text\\nCompany(t-5) → STLA(t)\\nCompany(t-3) → STLA(t)\\nCompany(t-2) → STLA(t)\\nCompany(t-1) → STLA(t)\\n\\nSTLA(t) → Company(t+1)\\nSTLA(t) → Company(t+3)\\nSTLA(t) → Company(t+5)\\n```\\n\\nReport observed lead/lag relationships.\\n\\nDo not treat them as guaranteed predictive signals.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 15. PHASE 10 — REGIME DETECTION\\n\\nDetect changing relationships over time.\\n\\nCalculate rolling relationships and identify periods where:\\n\\n- correlation strengthens\\n- correlation weakens\\n- beta changes\\n- volatility changes\\n- supply-chain exposure changes\\n\\nExample:\\n\\n```text\\n2022:\\nCorrelation = X\\n\\n2023:\\nCorrelation = Y\\n\\n2024:\\nCorrelation = Z\\n```\\n\\nDetect relationship regime changes.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 16. PHASE 11 — EVENT FINGERPRINTING\\n\\nCreate historical fingerprints for STLA events.\\n\\nEach event should contain:\\n\\n```text\\nevent_type\\nseverity\\nSTLA_abnormal_return\\nsector_reaction\\nmarket_reaction\\nsupplier_reaction\\ncompetitor_reaction\\ncommodity_reaction\\nrecovery_time\\n```\\n\\nWhen a new event occurs:\\n\\nFind historically similar events.\\n\\nOutput:\\n\\n```text\\nCurrent Event\\n\\nSimilar Event 1\\nSimilarity:\\nHistorical reaction:\\n\\nSimilar Event 2\\nSimilarity:\\nHistorical reaction:\\n```\\n\\nDo not use historical similarity as a price prediction mechanism.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 17. PHASE 12 — VECTOR SEARCH\\n\\nCreate embeddings for:\\n\\n- news\\n- filings\\n- company reports\\n- earnings materials\\n- event descriptions\\n- relationship evidence\\n- historical event summaries\\n\\nUse:\\n\\n- Sentence Transformers\\n- FAISS or Chroma\\n\\nPipeline:\\n\\n```text\\nDocument\\n→ chunk\\n→ embedding\\n→ vector store\\n→ retrieval\\n```\\n\\nEverything should run locally.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 18. PHASE 13 — LOCAL RAG\\n\\nIntegrate:\\n\\n- Ollama\\n- local open-source LLM\\n- local embeddings\\n- FAISS/Chroma\\n\\nThe RAG system should answer:\\n\\n```text\\nWhy did Company A potentially react to this STLA event?\\n\\nWhat companies are connected to Stellantis through batteries?\\n\\nWhat evidence supports the relationship between STLA and Company X?\\n\\nFind historical events similar to this event.\\n\\nWas this movement more STLA-specific or sector-wide?\\n```\\n\\nThe AI must:\\n\\n- cite evidence\\n- distinguish facts from hypotheses\\n- distinguish correlation from causation\\n- acknowledge uncertainty\\n- never invent sources\\n- never fabricate relationships\\n- never provide investment recommendations\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 19. PHASE 14 — AUTONOMOUS INVESTIGATION AGENT\\n\\nBuild an agent that automatically investigates abnormal STLA events.\\n\\nTrigger:\\n\\n```text\\nSTLA abnormal movement detected\\n```\\n\\nAgent workflow:\\n\\n```text\\nDetect event\\n      ↓\\nCheck market\\n      ↓\\nCheck sector\\n      ↓\\nSearch news\\n      ↓\\nClassify event\\n      ↓\\nQuery corporate graph\\n      ↓\\nFind exposed companies\\n      ↓\\nRun historical analysis\\n      ↓\\nFind similar events\\n      ↓\\nRetrieve filings/evidence\\n      ↓\\nGenerate report\\n```\\n\\nUse explicit tools/functions.\\n\\nDo not give the LLM unrestricted database access.\\n\\nLog:\\n\\n- tool calls\\n- queries\\n- retrieved documents\\n- calculations\\n- final reasoning inputs\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 20. PHASE 15 — SECOND-ORDER IMPACT ANALYSIS\\n\\nBuild indirect exposure analysis.\\n\\nExample:\\n\\n```text\\nSTLA\\n ↓\\nSupplier A\\n ↓\\nSupplier B\\n ↓\\nCommodity C\\n```\\n\\nIdentify:\\n\\n- first-order exposure\\n- second-order exposure\\n- third-order exposure\\n\\nFor each path show:\\n\\n```text\\nSTLA\\n→ Supplier A\\n→ Supplier B\\n\\nRelationship 1:\\nEvidence\\n\\nRelationship 2:\\nEvidence\\n```\\n\\nNever invent a path.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 21. PHASE 16 — SUPPLY-CHAIN CONCENTRATION\\n\\nCalculate analytical exposure profiles.\\n\\nFor each supplier/company:\\n\\n```text\\nrelationship strength\\nproduct criticality\\ngeographic concentration\\nalternative supplier availability\\nhistorical STLA sensitivity\\nrelationship duration\\ndocumented revenue exposure where available\\n```\\n\\nDo not fabricate revenue exposure.\\n\\nIf unavailable:\\n\\n```text\\nData unavailable\\n```\\n\\nDo not estimate without explicitly labeling the estimate.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 22. PHASE 17 — GEOGRAPHIC \u0026 COMMODITY ANALYSIS\\n\\nAdd:\\n\\n## Geographic exposure\\n\\nAnalyze:\\n\\n- Europe\\n- North America\\n- Asia\\n- other relevant regions\\n\\n## Commodity exposure\\n\\nWhere relevant:\\n\\n- lithium\\n- nickel\\n- cobalt\\n- copper\\n- aluminum\\n- steel\\n- oil\\n\\nConnect:\\n\\n```text\\nCommodity\\n→ Automotive cost structure\\n→ STLA\\n→ Suppliers\\n```\\n\\nDo not claim direct financial causation without evidence.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 23. PHASE 18 — MARKET ANOMALY DETECTION\\n\\nDetect unusual events automatically.\\n\\nExample:\\n\\n```text\\nExpected STLA daily volatility: ±2%\\n\\nActual movement: -8%\\n\\nANOMALY DETECTED\\n```\\n\\nWhen an anomaly occurs, automatically start an investigation.\\n\\nThe anomaly system should consider:\\n\\n- return\\n- volume\\n- volatility\\n- market movement\\n- sector movement\\n- news availability\\n- options data if legally/free available\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 24. PHASE 19 — CROSS-ASSET ANALYSIS\\n\\nWhere free data permits, analyze:\\n\\n- commodities\\n- FX\\n- market indices\\n- automotive ETFs\\n- relevant macro indicators\\n\\nCompare:\\n\\n```text\\nMacro movement\\n→ sector\\n→ STLA\\n→ ecosystem\\n```\\n\\nDo not make investment predictions.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 25. PHASE 20 — DASHBOARD\\n\\nBuild a professional Streamlit dashboard.\\n\\n## Page 1 — Market Overview\\n\\nDisplay:\\n\\n- STLA price\\n- daily return\\n- volatility\\n- current anomaly\\n- market movement\\n- sector movement\\n\\n## Page 2 — Event Explorer\\n\\nDisplay:\\n\\n- event timeline\\n- event severity\\n- event type\\n- STLA reaction\\n- market reaction\\n- sector reaction\\n\\n## Page 3 — Impact Analysis\\n\\nDisplay:\\n\\n```text\\nCompany\\nTicker\\nRelationship\\nGraph distance\\nCorrelation\\nBeta\\nHistorical event sensitivity\\nCurrent reaction\\nEvidence strength\\n```\\n\\n## Page 4 — Corporate Network\\n\\nInteractive graph.\\n\\n## Page 5 — Historical Events\\n\\nSearch similar events.\\n\\n## Page 6 — AI Investigation\\n\\nNatural-language interface.\\n\\nExample:\\n\\n```text\\nWhat companies should I investigate after this STLA event?\\n```\\n\\n## Page 7 — Evidence\\n\\nDisplay:\\n\\n- source\\n- filing\\n- article\\n- relationship evidence\\n- event evidence\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 26. PHASE 21 — FASTAPI\\n\\nCreate a backend API.\\n\\nEndpoints:\\n\\n```text\\nGET /health\\nGET /ready\\n\\nGET /companies\\nGET /companies/{ticker}\\n\\nGET /prices/{ticker}\\n\\nGET /events\\nGET /events/{event_id}\\n\\nGET /events/{event_id}/impact\\n\\nGET /relationships\\nGET /relationships/{company}\\n\\nGET /network\\n\\nGET /historical-events\\n\\nPOST /investigate\\n\\nPOST /search\\n```\\n\\nUse:\\n\\n- Pydantic\\n- validation\\n- structured errors\\n- pagination\\n- CORS\\n- logging\\n- OpenAPI documentation\\n\\nDo not put business logic in route handlers.\\n\\nUse service/repository architecture.\\n\\nThe dashboard should communicate with the API.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 27. PHASE 22 — DOCKER\\n\\nContainerize the project.\\n\\nCreate containers for:\\n\\n- API\\n- dashboard\\n- ingestion\\n- AI service if necessary\\n\\nCreate:\\n\\n```text\\ndocker-compose.yml\\n```\\n\\nSupport:\\n\\n```bash\\ndocker compose up\\n```\\n\\nImplement:\\n\\n- health checks\\n- volumes\\n- environment variables\\n- persistent local data\\n- service networking\\n\\nThe entire system must remain runnable locally.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 28. PHASE 23 — OBSERVABILITY\\n\\nAdd:\\n\\n- OpenTelemetry\\n- Prometheus\\n- Grafana\\n- structured logging\\n\\nMonitor:\\n\\n## API\\n\\n- request count\\n- latency\\n- errors\\n- throughput\\n\\n## Data pipeline\\n\\n- records processed\\n- ingestion duration\\n- failed records\\n- missing data\\n- stale data\\n\\n## AI\\n\\n- retrieval latency\\n- LLM latency\\n- investigation duration\\n- failed requests\\n\\nImplement correlation IDs.\\n\\nA single investigation should be traceable:\\n\\n```text\\nAPI\\n→ Event Engine\\n→ DuckDB\\n→ Knowledge Graph\\n→ RAG\\n→ LLM\\n→ Report\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 29. PHASE 24 — KUBERNETES\\n\\nDeploy locally to Kubernetes.\\n\\nUse:\\n\\n- Kubernetes\\n- Docker\\n- Helm where useful\\n\\nCreate:\\n\\n- Deployments\\n- Services\\n- ConfigMaps\\n- Secrets\\n- PersistentVolumes\\n- liveness probes\\n- readiness probes\\n- resource requests\\n- resource limits\\n\\nKeep the application runnable with Docker Compose.\\n\\nDo not make Kubernetes mandatory for local development.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 30. PHASE 25 — TERRAFORM\\n\\nCreate optional infrastructure-as-code.\\n\\nTerraform should define:\\n\\n- networking\\n- compute\\n- storage\\n- Kubernetes infrastructure\\n- monitoring infrastructure\\n\\nUse variables.\\n\\nAvoid hardcoded cloud credentials.\\n\\nSupport:\\n\\n```bash\\nterraform init\\nterraform plan\\nterraform apply\\nterraform destroy\\n```\\n\\nCloud deployment should remain optional because the core project must remain zero-cost.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 31. PHASE 26 — CI/CD\\n\\nCreate GitHub Actions.\\n\\nOn pull request:\\n\\n```text\\nInstall dependencies\\n↓\\nLint\\n↓\\nUnit tests\\n↓\\nIntegration tests\\n↓\\nSecurity/dependency checks\\n↓\\nDocker build\\n```\\n\\nOn main:\\n\\n```text\\nBuild\\n↓\\nTest\\n↓\\nPackage\\n↓\\nPublish artifacts where configured\\n```\\n\\nNever commit:\\n\\n- API keys\\n- credentials\\n- tokens\\n- secrets\\n- private data\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 32. PHASE 27 — AUTOMATED INVESTIGATION REPORT\\n\\nWhen an abnormal STLA event occurs, generate:\\n\\n```text\\n==================================================\\nSTELLANTIS MARKET IMPACT REPORT\\n==================================================\\n\\nEVENT\\n\\nDate:\\nEvent type:\\nSeverity:\\nSTLA movement:\\n\\nMARKET CONTEXT\\n\\nMarket movement:\\nAutomotive sector movement:\\nSTLA abnormal return:\\n\\nEVENT INFORMATION\\n\\nLikely catalyst:\\nSupporting evidence:\\n\\nPOTENTIALLY EXPOSED COMPANIES\\n\\nCompany:\\nTicker:\\nRelationship:\\nGraph distance:\\nHistorical correlation:\\nHistorical event sensitivity:\\nCurrent movement:\\n\\nBUSINESS RELATIONSHIP\\n\\nDocumented relationship:\\nEvidence:\\nSource:\\n\\nHISTORICAL ANALOGUES\\n\\nSimilar event:\\nSimilarity:\\nHistorical STLA reaction:\\nHistorical ecosystem reaction:\\n\\nSECOND-ORDER EXPOSURE\\n\\nCompany:\\nConnection:\\nEvidence:\\n\\nUNCERTAINTIES\\n\\nKnown facts:\\nObserved statistical relationships:\\nDocumented relationships:\\nAI hypotheses:\\nUnknowns:\\n\\nSOURCES\\n```\\n\\nThe report must distinguish facts from inference.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 33. FINAL USER EXPERIENCE\\n\\nThe main dashboard should answer one central question:\\n\\n\u003e \\\"Something happened to Stellantis. What else should I investigate?\\\"\\n\\nThe workflow should be:\\n\\n```text\\nSTLA abnormal movement\\n        ↓\\nEvent detection\\n        ↓\\nMarket/sector comparison\\n        ↓\\nNews investigation\\n        ↓\\nEvent classification\\n        ↓\\nCorporate relationship graph\\n        ↓\\nFirst/second/third-order exposure\\n        ↓\\nHistorical event analysis\\n        ↓\\nSimilar-event search\\n        ↓\\nSupporting filings/news\\n        ↓\\nAI investigation\\n        ↓\\nEvidence-backed report\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 34. EXAMPLE QUESTIONS THE SYSTEM MUST SUPPORT\\n\\nThe AI interface should eventually understand questions such as:\\n\\n```text\\nWhich companies historically move with Stellantis?\\n\\nWhich suppliers have the strongest documented relationships with Stellantis?\\n\\nWhat happened during the last major Stellantis production event?\\n\\nWas that movement specific to Stellantis or part of a broader automotive-sector movement?\\n\\nWhich battery companies are connected to Stellantis?\\n\\nShow companies within two degrees of Stellantis.\\n\\nWhy did Company X move when Stellantis moved?\\n\\nFind historical events similar to today's event.\\n\\nWhich supplier relationships have changed over time?\\n\\nWhat companies were exposed to Stellantis during this production disruption?\\n\\nShow second-order supply-chain exposure.\\n\\nWhat evidence supports the relationship between Stellantis and Company X?\\n\\nWhat changed compared with the previous earnings event?\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 35. QUALITY REQUIREMENTS\\n\\nThe implementation must be:\\n\\n- modular\\n- testable\\n- documented\\n- reproducible\\n- configurable\\n- observable\\n- containerized\\n- version controlled\\n\\nAvoid:\\n\\n- giant Python files\\n- hardcoded tickers\\n- hardcoded paths\\n- hardcoded API keys\\n- duplicated logic\\n- undocumented assumptions\\n- fabricated data\\n- fabricated relationships\\n- fabricated financial statistics\\n- unsupported causal claims\\n\\nUse type hints.\\n\\nUse docstrings for important functions.\\n\\nUse structured logging.\\n\\nWrite tests alongside features.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 36. DEVELOPMENT STRATEGY\\n\\nDo NOT attempt to implement everything at once.\\n\\nImplement in this order:\\n\\n```text\\nPHASE 1\\nMarket data\\n\\n↓\\n\\nPHASE 2\\nEvent detection\\n\\n↓\\n\\nPHASE 3\\nCorrelation / event studies\\n\\n↓\\n\\nPHASE 4\\nMarket vs sector analysis\\n\\n↓\\n\\nPHASE 5\\nNews intelligence\\n\\n↓\\n\\nPHASE 6\\nCorporate knowledge graph\\n\\n↓\\n\\nPHASE 7\\nSupply-chain impact analysis\\n\\n↓\\n\\nPHASE 8\\nAdvanced statistics\\n\\n↓\\n\\nPHASE 9\\nLead/lag + regime analysis\\n\\n↓\\n\\nPHASE 10\\nEvent fingerprinting\\n\\n↓\\n\\nPHASE 11\\nVector database\\n\\n↓\\n\\nPHASE 12\\nRAG\\n\\n↓\\n\\nPHASE 13\\nAI investigation agent\\n\\n↓\\n\\nPHASE 14\\nDashboard\\n\\n↓\\n\\nPHASE 15\\nFastAPI\\n\\n↓\\n\\nPHASE 16\\nDocker\\n\\n↓\\n\\nPHASE 17\\nObservability\\n\\n↓\\n\\nPHASE 18\\nKubernetes\\n\\n↓\\n\\nPHASE 19\\nTerraform\\n\\n↓\\n\\nPHASE 20\\nCI/CD\\n```\\n\\nAfter completing each phase:\\n\\n1. Run tests.\\n2. Verify existing functionality.\\n3. Do not break previous phases.\\n4. Update README.\\n5. Update architecture documentation.\\n6. Update database migrations/schema if necessary.\\n7. Commit the working phase.\\n8. Only then proceed to the next phase.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 37. combines:\\n\\n**Financial Analytics + Event Studies + Supply-Chain Intelligence + Knowledge Graphs + RAG + Autonomous AI Investigation + Cloud Infrastructure**\\n\\nThe system does NOT attempt to tell users what stocks to buy or sell.\\n\\nIts purpose is to answer:\\n\\n\u003e **\\\"When something materially affects Stellantis, how does that event propagate through its broader corporate and I’d build it as **one project with progressively more sophisticated phases**, rather than trying to generate the entire system at once.\\n\\nUse these prompts sequentially with your coding AI/agent. Each phase should build on the previous one and **not rewrite working components unnecessarily**.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# Phase 0 — Project specification\\n\\n```text\\nI am building a free, open-source project called:\\n\\n\\\"Stellantis Market Impact Intelligence\\\"\\n\\nThe goal is to analyze how events affecting Stellantis (STLA) propagate through the automotive ecosystem.\\n\\nThe system should eventually be able to answer:\\n\\n\\\"When something materially affects Stellantis, which other publicly traded companies are potentially affected, how strongly have they historically reacted, what business relationship connects them, and what evidence explains the relationship?\\\"\\n\\nIMPORTANT CONSTRAINTS:\\n\\n- Entire project should be buildable at ₹0 cost.\\n- Prefer local/open-source tools.\\n- Do not use paid APIs.\\n- Do not require cloud infrastructure initially.\\n- Use public/free data sources where legally and technically appropriate.\\n- LLM should eventually run locally using Ollama.\\n- Store data locally using Parquet and DuckDB.\\n- Use Python for data processing.\\n- Use FastAPI for the backend.\\n- Use Streamlit initially for the dashboard.\\n- Use Docker for reproducibility.\\n- Kubernetes/Terraform/observability will be added in later phases.\\n\\nTECH STACK:\\n\\nPython\\nPandas/Polars\\nDuckDB\\nParquet\\nSciPy\\nStatsmodels\\nScikit-learn\\nNetworkX\\nFAISS or Chroma\\nSentence Transformers\\nOllama\\nFastAPI\\nStreamlit\\nDocker\\nGitHub Actions\\nOpenTelemetry\\nPrometheus\\nGrafana\\nKubernetes\\nTerraform\\n\\nDo NOT build the entire application yet.\\n\\nFirst produce:\\n\\n1. System architecture\\n2. Repository structure\\n3. Data model\\n4. Major services/modules\\n5. Dependencies\\n6. Development phases\\n7. Interface contracts between modules\\n8. Testing strategy\\n9. Configuration/environment strategy\\n\\nThe architecture must allow each phase to be implemented independently without major rewrites later.\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# Phase 1 — Market data foundation\\n\\nThis gets the basic project working.\\n\\n```text\\nImplement Phase 1 of the Stellantis Market Impact Intelligence project.\\n\\nOBJECTIVE:\\n\\nBuild the market-data ingestion and storage layer.\\n\\nFor the initial implementation, focus ONLY on historical stock-price data.\\n\\nPrimary company:\\n\\nStellantis — STLA\\n\\nAlso create a configurable universe of related companies, initially including major automotive OEMs and a small set of automotive/technology companies.\\n\\nDo not hardcode the architecture around only STLA.\\n\\nREQUIREMENTS:\\n\\n1. Create a Python market-data ingestion module.\\n\\n2. Use free/public market-data sources such as yfinance where appropriate.\\n\\n3. Retrieve:\\n   - Open\\n   - High\\n   - Low\\n   - Close\\n   - Adjusted close\\n   - Volume\\n\\n4. Support configurable:\\n   - ticker\\n   - start date\\n   - end date\\n   - interval\\n\\n5. Save raw data as Parquet.\\n\\n6. Create a DuckDB database.\\n\\n7. Create normalized tables:\\n   - companies\\n   - price_data\\n\\n8. Include:\\n   - ticker\\n   - company name\\n   - exchange\\n   - sector if available\\n   - date\\n   - OHLCV data\\n\\n9. Implement data validation:\\n   - duplicate dates\\n   - missing values\\n   - invalid prices\\n   - missing trading days\\n   - API failures\\n\\n10. Implement structured logging.\\n\\n11. Implement configuration using environment variables/config files rather than hardcoded values.\\n\\n12. Write unit tests.\\n\\n13. Create a CLI such as:\\n\\npython -m ingestion.market_data --ticker STLA\\n\\n14. Add README documentation explaining setup and usage.\\n\\nIMPORTANT:\\n\\nDo not build the AI layer, dashboard, knowledge graph, or Kubernetes yet.\\n\\nKeep the implementation modular because later phases will consume this data.\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# Phase 2 — Event detection\\n\\nNow teach the system what a **significant STLA movement** is.\\n\\n```text\\nImplement Phase 2 of Stellantis Market Impact Intelligence.\\n\\nOBJECTIVE:\\n\\nBuild an event-detection engine that identifies statistically unusual movements in STLA.\\n\\nINPUT:\\n\\nUse the Phase 1 DuckDB market database.\\n\\nFEATURES:\\n\\nCalculate:\\n\\n- Daily return\\n- Log return\\n- Rolling volatility\\n- Rolling mean\\n- Rolling standard deviation\\n- Z-score\\n- Drawdown\\n- Volume anomaly\\n\\nEVENT DETECTION:\\n\\nImplement configurable detection methods:\\n\\n1. Absolute return threshold\\n2. Volatility-adjusted return\\n3. Z-score\\n4. Abnormal volume\\n5. Combined event score\\n\\nAn event should contain:\\n\\n- event_id\\n- ticker\\n- event_date\\n- return\\n- z_score\\n- volume_z_score\\n- volatility\\n- severity\\n- direction\\n- detection_method\\n\\nAvoid arbitrary hardcoded thresholds where possible.\\n\\nMake thresholds configurable.\\n\\nStore events in DuckDB.\\n\\nCreate tests for:\\n- positive shock\\n- negative shock\\n- normal movement\\n- high-volume event\\n- missing data\\n\\nAlso create a command:\\n\\npython -m analytics.detect_events\\n\\nThe output should show the detected STLA events.\\n\\nDo NOT yet determine why an event happened.\\n\\nDo NOT build an AI explanation system.\\n\\nDo NOT make stock-price predictions.\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# Phase 3 — Correlation \u0026 impact analysis\\n\\nThis is the core of your original idea.\\n\\n```text\\nImplement Phase 3.\\n\\nOBJECTIVE:\\n\\nDetermine which publicly traded companies historically show relationships with Stellantis stock movements.\\n\\nFor every company in the configured universe, calculate:\\n\\n1. Pearson correlation\\n2. Spearman correlation\\n3. Rolling correlation\\n4. Beta relative to STLA\\n5. R²\\n6. Statistical significance\\n7. Sample size\\n\\nThen perform EVENT-WINDOW analysis around every detected STLA event.\\n\\nFor each event calculate company returns:\\n\\n- Event day\\n- +1 trading day\\n- +3 trading days\\n- +5 trading days\\n\\nAlso calculate:\\n\\n- cumulative return\\n- abnormal return\\n- sector-adjusted return where data is available\\n- market-adjusted return\\n\\nIMPORTANT:\\n\\nDo not claim that correlation means causation.\\n\\nSeparate:\\n\\n\\\"Observed statistical relationship\\\"\\n\\nfrom\\n\\n\\\"Documented business relationship.\\\"\\n\\nCreate tables:\\n\\ncompanies\\ncorrelations\\nrolling_correlations\\nevent_returns\\nabnormal_returns\\n\\nCreate reusable Python functions rather than one giant script.\\n\\nAdd tests.\\n\\nCreate CLI commands:\\n\\npython -m analytics.correlation\\npython -m analytics.event_study\\n\\nProduce CSV/Parquet outputs and DuckDB tables.\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# Phase 4 — Market vs sector vs STLA-specific\\n\\nThis phase makes your analysis much more credible.\\n\\n```text\\nImplement Phase 4.\\n\\nOBJECTIVE:\\n\\nDetermine whether a major Stellantis movement appears to be:\\n\\nA. STLA-specific\\nB. Automotive-sector-wide\\nC. Broader market-wide\\nD. Mixed\\n\\nAdd configurable benchmark assets:\\n\\n- broad market index\\n- automotive sector index/ETF\\n- relevant regional index\\n\\nFor each STLA event calculate:\\n\\nSTLA return\\nMarket return\\nSector return\\nSTLA abnormal return\\nSector abnormal return\\nMarket-adjusted return\\n\\nCreate an event classification engine.\\n\\nExample output:\\n\\nEvent:\\n2026-XX-XX\\n\\nSTLA:\\n-7.2%\\n\\nAutomotive sector:\\n-1.4%\\n\\nMarket:\\n-0.6%\\n\\nInterpretation:\\nSTLA experienced a substantially larger move than the broader sector and market.\\n\\nIMPORTANT:\\n\\nDo not make causal claims.\\n\\nClearly distinguish numerical measurements from interpretations.\\n\\nAdd statistical tests where appropriate.\\n\\nStore all results in DuckDB.\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# Phase 5 — News \u0026 event intelligence\\n\\nNow answer **“why did STLA move?”**\\n\\n```text\\nImplement Phase 5.\\n\\nOBJECTIVE:\\n\\nBuild a free news/event ingestion pipeline.\\n\\nUse legally accessible free/public sources such as RSS feeds and publicly available company/news sources.\\n\\nDO NOT use paid news APIs.\\n\\nPipeline:\\n\\nRSS/news source\\n→ ingestion\\n→ deduplication\\n→ normalization\\n→ article metadata\\n→ local storage\\n→ DuckDB\\n\\nStore:\\n\\n- article_id\\n- timestamp\\n- title\\n- source\\n- URL\\n- summary/content where legally available\\n- mentioned companies\\n- ticker\\n- publication time\\n\\nBuild an event classifier that categorizes articles into:\\n\\n- Earnings\\n- Production\\n- Factory\\n- Supply chain\\n- Partnership\\n- Acquisition\\n- Regulation\\n- Recall\\n- Product\\n- EV strategy\\n- Battery\\n- Management\\n- Labor\\n- Geopolitical\\n- Macro\\n- Other\\n\\nFor each detected STLA event:\\n\\nSearch the relevant time window and identify potentially related news.\\n\\nRank articles based on:\\n\\n- timestamp proximity\\n- STLA mention\\n- event relevance\\n- source reliability\\n\\nDo not yet use an LLM.\\n\\nFirst implement deterministic/rule-based classification.\\n\\nAdd tests and logging.\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# Phase 6 — Company relationship graph\\n\\nThis is where it becomes much more than stock analysis.\\n\\n```text\\nImplement Phase 6.\\n\\nOBJECTIVE:\\n\\nCreate a Stellantis corporate relationship graph.\\n\\nUse NetworkX initially.\\n\\nEntities:\\n\\n- Companies\\n- Stellantis brands\\n- Suppliers\\n- Partners\\n- Competitors\\n- Technologies\\n- Products\\n- Manufacturing facilities\\n- Geographic regions\\n\\nRelationship types:\\n\\n- supplier\\n- customer\\n- partner\\n- competitor\\n- technology_partner\\n- joint_venture\\n- manufacturing\\n- logistics\\n- battery_supplier\\n- semiconductor_supplier\\n- cloud_provider\\n- software_partner\\n\\nEach relationship must contain:\\n\\n- source\\n- evidence URL\\n- date discovered\\n- relationship start date if known\\n- relationship end date if known\\n- confidence\\n- description\\n\\nIMPORTANT:\\n\\nNever invent relationships.\\n\\nEvery relationship should have supporting evidence.\\n\\nCreate:\\n\\n1. Graph construction module\\n2. Graph query module\\n3. Relationship database schema\\n4. Network visualization\\n5. Tests\\n\\nSupport queries such as:\\n\\n\\\"Show all direct suppliers of Stellantis.\\\"\\n\\n\\\"Show companies within two relationship hops of Stellantis.\\\"\\n\\n\\\"Show all battery-related relationships.\\\"\\n\\nStore relationship data separately from market data.\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# Phase 7 — Supply-chain impact propagation\\n\\nNow connect the graph to your stock analytics.\\n\\n```text\\nImplement Phase 7.\\n\\nOBJECTIVE:\\n\\nCombine the market-impact engine with the corporate relationship graph.\\n\\nWhen a significant STLA event occurs:\\n\\n1. Identify event type.\\n2. Identify potentially affected business areas.\\n3. Traverse the relationship graph.\\n4. Identify directly and indirectly connected companies.\\n5. Retrieve their historical stock behavior.\\n6. Compare historical sensitivity with the current event.\\n\\nClassify companies into:\\n\\nFIRST ORDER:\\nDirect relationship with STLA.\\n\\nSECOND ORDER:\\nConnected through another company/entity.\\n\\nTHIRD ORDER:\\nIndirect ecosystem exposure.\\n\\nFor each company calculate an analytical exposure profile:\\n\\n- relationship type\\n- graph distance\\n- historical correlation\\n- event-window sensitivity\\n- frequency of reaction\\n- evidence strength\\n\\nDo NOT predict future stock prices.\\n\\nThe output should answer:\\n\\n\\\"Which companies are worth investigating when this type of STLA event occurs?\\\"\\n\\nUse analytical language, not investment recommendations.\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# Phase 8 — RAG + local AI\\n\\nNow introduce your local LLM.\\n\\n```text\\nImplement Phase 8.\\n\\nOBJECTIVE:\\n\\nBuild a completely local RAG system for Stellantis market intelligence.\\n\\nUse:\\n\\n- Ollama\\n- local open-source LLM\\n- Sentence Transformers\\n- FAISS or Chroma\\n\\nDocuments:\\n\\n- company filings\\n- public company reports\\n- earnings transcripts where legally accessible\\n- public announcements\\n- collected news\\n- relationship evidence\\n- historical event summaries\\n\\nPipeline:\\n\\nDocument\\n→ chunk\\n→ embedding\\n→ vector database\\n→ retrieval\\n→ local LLM\\n\\nThe AI must answer questions such as:\\n\\n\\\"Why did Supplier A potentially react to this STLA event?\\\"\\n\\n\\\"What companies are connected to Stellantis through batteries?\\\"\\n\\n\\\"Find historical events similar to this one.\\\"\\n\\n\\\"What evidence supports the relationship between STLA and Company X?\\\"\\n\\nIMPORTANT AI RULES:\\n\\n- Never invent evidence.\\n- Cite retrieved sources.\\n- Distinguish facts from hypotheses.\\n- Never claim correlation proves causation.\\n- Say when evidence is insufficient.\\n- Never provide investment recommendations.\\n\\nCreate an AI service abstraction so the LLM provider can later be changed without rewriting the application.\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# Phase 9 — Similar-event engine\\n\\nThis is one of the features I'd definitely add.\\n\\n```text\\nImplement Phase 9.\\n\\nOBJECTIVE:\\n\\nBuild a historical event similarity engine.\\n\\nFor every historical STLA event create an event representation containing:\\n\\n- event type\\n- event description\\n- STLA return\\n- sector return\\n- market return\\n- affected companies\\n- affected industries\\n- news embeddings\\n- relationship graph features\\n\\nUse embeddings to identify similar historical events.\\n\\nGiven a new event:\\n\\n1. Generate its embedding.\\n2. Search the historical event vector database.\\n3. Return the most similar historical events.\\n4. Compare their market reactions.\\n5. Compare affected companies.\\n6. Provide supporting evidence.\\n\\nOutput:\\n\\nCURRENT EVENT\\n\\nSIMILAR HISTORICAL EVENTS\\n\\nEVENT 1\\nSimilarity:\\nSTLA reaction:\\nSupplier reactions:\\n\\nEVENT 2\\nSimilarity:\\nSTLA reaction:\\nSupplier reactions:\\n\\nDo not use this system to predict future prices.\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# Phase 10 — Autonomous investigation agent\\n\\nThis is your AI showcase.\\n\\n```text\\nImplement Phase 10.\\n\\nOBJECTIVE:\\n\\nCreate an autonomous Stellantis event-investigation agent.\\n\\nTrigger:\\n\\nSTLA experiences an abnormal market event.\\n\\nThe agent should automatically:\\n\\n1. Detect the event.\\n2. Check market and sector movements.\\n3. Search relevant news.\\n4. Classify the event.\\n5. Query the corporate relationship graph.\\n6. Identify potentially exposed companies.\\n7. Retrieve historical event analogues.\\n8. Calculate historical reactions.\\n9. Retrieve supporting filings/news.\\n10. Generate an investigation report.\\n\\nThe agent should produce:\\n\\nEVENT\\nWHY IT MATTERS\\nMARKET CONTEXT\\nPOTENTIALLY EXPOSED COMPANIES\\nBUSINESS RELATIONSHIPS\\nHISTORICAL EVIDENCE\\nSIMILAR EVENTS\\nUNCERTAINTIES\\nSOURCE EVIDENCE\\n\\nThe agent must not provide trading recommendations or price predictions.\\n\\nImplement the agent using explicit tools/functions rather than giving the LLM unrestricted access to the entire system.\\n\\nLog every tool call and intermediate result.\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# Phase 11 — Dashboard\\n\\n```text\\nImplement Phase 11.\\n\\nOBJECTIVE:\\n\\nBuild the first complete user-facing dashboard using Streamlit.\\n\\nPages:\\n\\n1. MARKET OVERVIEW\\n\\nDisplay:\\n- STLA price\\n- daily return\\n- volatility\\n- current detected events\\n- market/sector comparison\\n\\n2. EVENT EXPLORER\\n\\nDisplay:\\n- event timeline\\n- event severity\\n- event type\\n- STLA reaction\\n- sector reaction\\n- market reaction\\n\\n3. IMPACT ANALYSIS\\n\\nFor each event show:\\n- potentially exposed companies\\n- historical correlation\\n- beta\\n- event-window reaction\\n- relationship type\\n- evidence\\n\\n4. CORPORATE NETWORK\\n\\nInteractive relationship graph.\\n\\n5. HISTORICAL EVENTS\\n\\nSearch similar events.\\n\\n6. AI INVESTIGATION\\n\\nNatural-language interface.\\n\\nExample:\\n\\n\\\"What companies were affected by the last major STLA production event?\\\"\\n\\n7. EVIDENCE\\n\\nShow:\\n- source\\n- article\\n- filing\\n- relationship evidence\\n\\nKeep the UI professional and suitable for a corporate technology demonstration.\\n\\nDo not add investment recommendations.\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# Phase 12 — FastAPI backend\\n\\nNow separate your frontend and backend.\\n\\n```text\\nImplement Phase 12.\\n\\nOBJECTIVE:\\n\\nMove application functionality behind a FastAPI backend.\\n\\nCreate APIs for:\\n\\nGET /companies\\nGET /companies/{ticker}\\nGET /prices/{ticker}\\nGET /events\\nGET /events/{event_id}\\nGET /events/{event_id}/impact\\nGET /relationships\\nGET /relationships/{company}\\nGET /network\\nGET /historical-events\\nPOST /investigate\\nPOST /search\\n\\nRequirements:\\n\\n- Pydantic models\\n- input validation\\n- structured errors\\n- logging\\n- API documentation\\n- pagination\\n- configurable CORS\\n- health endpoint\\n- readiness endpoint\\n\\nDo not place business logic directly inside route handlers.\\n\\nUse service/repository architecture.\\n\\nThe Streamlit frontend should consume the API rather than directly querying the database.\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# Phase 13 — Docker\\n\\n```text\\nImplement Phase 13.\\n\\nContainerize the complete application.\\n\\nCreate Dockerfiles for:\\n\\n- ingestion\\n- backend\\n- dashboard\\n- AI service if necessary\\n\\nCreate docker-compose.yml for local development.\\n\\nServices should include only what is actually necessary.\\n\\nThe system should be able to start with:\\n\\ndocker compose up\\n\\nDocument:\\n\\n- environment variables\\n- volumes\\n- networking\\n- health checks\\n- local data persistence\\n\\nDo not introduce Kubernetes yet.\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# Phase 14 — Observability\\n\\nThis is where your Cloud Developer profile becomes visible.\\n\\n```text\\nImplement Phase 14.\\n\\nAdd production-style observability.\\n\\nUse:\\n\\n- OpenTelemetry\\n- Prometheus\\n- Grafana\\n- structured logging\\n\\nMonitor:\\n\\nAPPLICATION:\\n- API latency\\n- request count\\n- errors\\n- AI request duration\\n\\nDATA PIPELINES:\\n- ingestion duration\\n- records processed\\n- failed records\\n- stale data\\n- missing data\\n\\nAI:\\n- retrieval latency\\n- LLM latency\\n- token usage if available\\n- failed investigations\\n\\nCreate dashboards for system health.\\n\\nAdd correlation IDs so a single investigation can be traced across:\\n\\nAPI\\n→ analytics\\n→ database\\n→ graph\\n→ RAG\\n→ LLM\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# Phase 15 — Kubernetes\\n\\nOnly now move to Kubernetes.\\n\\n```text\\nImplement Phase 15.\\n\\nDeploy the application to a local Kubernetes cluster.\\n\\nUse:\\n\\n- Kubernetes\\n- Docker\\n- Helm if appropriate\\n\\nServices:\\n\\n- FastAPI\\n- dashboard\\n- ingestion worker\\n- AI service\\n- Neo4j if required\\n- observability components\\n\\nImplement:\\n\\n- Deployments\\n- Services\\n- ConfigMaps\\n- Secrets\\n- PersistentVolumes where required\\n- Liveness probes\\n- Readiness probes\\n- Resource requests/limits\\n- Horizontal scaling where appropriate\\n\\nThe application must remain runnable locally without Kubernetes.\\n\\nDocument the architecture and explain why each component is containerized separately.\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# Phase 16 — Terraform\\n\\n```text\\nImplement Phase 16.\\n\\nCreate Terraform infrastructure definitions for the project.\\n\\nThe infrastructure should be cloud-provider-neutral where practical.\\n\\nDefine:\\n\\n- networking\\n- compute\\n- storage\\n- Kubernetes infrastructure\\n- monitoring infrastructure\\n\\nKeep cloud deployment optional.\\n\\nThe local Docker/Kubernetes environment must remain the primary zero-cost development environment.\\n\\nUse variables instead of hardcoded infrastructure values.\\n\\nInclude:\\n\\nterraform init\\nterraform plan\\nterraform apply\\nterraform destroy\\n\\nDocument the infrastructure architecture.\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# Phase 17 — CI/CD\\n\\n```text\\nImplement Phase 17.\\n\\nCreate GitHub Actions CI/CD.\\n\\nOn pull request:\\n\\n1. Install dependencies.\\n2. Run linting.\\n3. Run unit tests.\\n4. Run integration tests.\\n5. Validate Docker builds.\\n6. Run security/dependency checks.\\n\\nOn main branch:\\n\\n1. Build Docker images.\\n2. Tag images.\\n3. Publish artifacts where configured.\\n4. Deploy only when deployment credentials/configuration are available.\\n\\nDo not require paid GitHub services.\\n\\nKeep secrets out of the repository.\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# Phase 18 — Final AI investigation report\\n\\nFinally, make the entire thing feel like a product.\\n\\n```text\\nImplement the final investigation-report system.\\n\\nWhen an STLA abnormal event is detected, generate a structured report:\\n\\n==================================================\\nSTELLANTIS MARKET IMPACT REPORT\\n==================================================\\n\\nEVENT\\nDate:\\nEvent type:\\nSTLA movement:\\n\\nMARKET CONTEXT\\nMarket movement:\\nAutomotive sector movement:\\nSTLA abnormal return:\\n\\nEVENT EXPLANATION\\nLikely catalyst:\\nSupporting sources:\\n\\nPOTENTIALLY EXPOSED COMPANIES\\n\\nCompany:\\nTicker:\\nRelationship:\\nGraph distance:\\nHistorical correlation:\\nHistorical event sensitivity:\\nCurrent movement:\\n\\nBUSINESS RELATIONSHIP\\n\\nDocumented relationship:\\nEvidence:\\nSource:\\n\\nHISTORICAL ANALOGUES\\n\\nSimilar event:\\nSimilarity:\\nHistorical STLA reaction:\\nHistorical ecosystem reaction:\\n\\nSECOND-ORDER EXPOSURE\\n\\nCompany:\\nConnection:\\nEvidence:\\n\\nUNCERTAINTIES\\n\\nClearly state:\\n- What is known\\n- What is statistically observed\\n- What is inferred\\n- What cannot be established\\n\\nSOURCES\\n\\nList all supporting sources.\\n\\nIMPORTANT:\\n\\nNever present an inferred relationship as an established fact.\\n\\nNever provide a buy/sell recommendation.\\n\\nNever claim that historical correlation guarantees future behavior.\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# The final architecture\\n\\nAfter all phases, you're effectively building this:\\n\\n```text\\n                       ┌───────────────┐\\n                       │ Market Data   │\\n                       └───────┬───────┘\\n                               │\\n                       ┌───────▼───────┐\\n                       │ Data Pipeline │\\n                       └───────┬───────┘\\n                               │\\n              ┌────────────────┼────────────────┐\\n              ▼                ▼                ▼\\n        DuckDB/Parquet    Event Engine    News/Filings\\n              │                │                │\\n              └────────────────┼────────────────┘\\n                               ▼\\n                    ┌────────────────────┐\\n                    │ Impact Analytics   │\\n                    └─────────┬──────────┘\\n                              │\\n              ┌───────────────┼────────────────┐\\n              ▼               ▼                ▼\\n       Correlation       Event Study      Anomaly Detection\\n              │               │                │\\n              └───────────────┼────────────────┘\\n                              ▼\\n                     ┌─────────────────┐\\n                     │ Knowledge Graph │\\n                     └────────┬────────┘\\n                              │\\n                    ┌─────────▼─────────┐\\n                    │ RAG / Vector DB   │\\n                    └─────────┬─────────┘\\n                              │\\n                    ┌─────────▼─────────┐\\n                    │ Local LLM / Agent │\\n                    └─────────┬─────────┘\\n                              │\\n                    ┌─────────▼─────────┐\\n                    │ FastAPI           │\\n                    └─────────┬─────────┘\\n                              │\\n                    ┌─────────▼─────────┐\\n                    │ Dashboard         │\\n                    └───────────────────┘\\n\\n        ───────── Infrastructure ─────────\\n        Docker → Kubernetes → Terraform\\n        OpenTelemetry → Prometheus → Grafana\\n        GitHub Actions → CI/CD\\n```\\n\\n### Don't build all of this at once\\n\\nYour **minimum impressive version** is actually just:\\n\\n**Phase 1 → 2 → 3 → 4 → 5 → 6 → 8 → 11**\\n\\nThat gets you:\\n\\n\u003e **Market data + event detection + statistical impact analysis + news intelligence + corporate knowledge graph + RAG + AI + dashboard.**\\n\\nThen **Docker/Kubernetes/Terraform/observability** become the infrastructure layer you add after the intelligence product works.\\n\\nThat separation is important: otherwise you'll spend weeks configuring Kubernetes before you have anything interesting to ":43,\"_240\":-5},{\"_92\":440,\"_238\":43,\"_239\":43,\"_240\":-5},{\"_92\":439,\"_238\":43,\"_239\":43,\"_240\":-5},{\"_92\":438,\"_238\":43,\"_239\":43,\"_240\":-5},{\"_92\":437,\"_238\":43,\"_239\":43,\"_240\":-5},{\"_92\":436,\"_238\":43,\"_239\":43,\"_240\":-5},{\"_92\":435,\"_238\":43,\"_239\":43,\"_240\":-5},{\"_92\":434,\"_238\":43,\"_239\":43,\"_240\":-5},{\"_92\":433,\"_238\":43,\"_239\":43,\"_240\":-5},{\"_92\":432,\"_238\":43,\"_239\":43,\"_240\":-5},{\"_92\":431,\"_238\":43,\"_239\":43,\"_240\":-5},\"izxoiy\",\"3cr1sb\",\"kwwpy2\",\"x1op77\",\"v3ep1l\",\"83kq1l\",\"spbfib\",\"8mo2tm\",\"17mz7y\",\"ux533c\",\"xb1pfq\",\"qmiwe3\",\"gy2a42\",\"horydm\",\"orlsz1\",\"lpdqkr\",\"s81gx2\",\"iq35k5\",\"r46g6v\",\"vhi90k\",\"wp5tcw\",\"vmplen\",\"ats9l7\",\"rbi84t\",\"7ximqv\",\"n0nm19\",\"zn0txb\",\"ulycbe\",\"cfvqr1\",\"ccdz7f\",\"p9k2na\",[293],[464],\"Yes. You can build almost the entire thing for **₹0**, especially because you already have a decent local machine. The main trick is to avoid paid market-data APIs and paid LLM APIs.\\n\\n## A ₹0 architecture\\n\\n```text\\n                FREE DATA SOURCES\\n                       │\\n       ┌───────────────┼────────────────┐\\n       ↓               ↓                ↓\\n   Stock Data        News          Company Filings\\n       │               │                │\\n       └───────────────┼────────────────┘\\n                       ↓\\n                Python ingestion\\n                       ↓\\n                 DuckDB / Parquet\\n                       ↓\\n              Pandas / Polars\\n                       ↓\\n       ┌───────────────┼────────────────┐\\n       ↓               ↓                ↓\\n  Event Engine   Correlation Engine   NLP\\n       │               │                │\\n       └───────────────┼────────────────┘\\n                       ↓\\n              Chroma / FAISS\\n                       ↓\\n              Knowledge Graph\\n                       ↓\\n             Local LLM / Ollama\\n                       ↓\\n                FastAPI\\n                       ↓\\n              Streamlit / React\\n```\\n\\n### 1. Stock data — $0\\n\\nFor historical research, don't pay for Bloomberg/Refinitiv-style data.\\n\\nUse free sources such as:\\n\\n- Yahoo Finance via `yfinance`\\n- Stooq\\n- Alpha Vantage free tier if you need an API\\n- Kaggle datasets for historical/backtesting datasets\\n\\nFor your initial version, I'd use:\\n\\n```bash\\npip install yfinance\\n```\\n\\nThen download:\\n\\n```text\\nSTLA\\nGM\\nF\\nTM\\nVWAGY\\nRNO.PA\\nBMW.DE\\nMBG.DE\\nTSLA\\n```\\n\\nplus relevant suppliers and sector indices.\\n\\n**Important:** free data sources can have licensing, rate-limit, and reliability limitations. For a portfolio project, that's fine if you clearly document the source and don't present it as institutional-grade market data.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 2. News — $0\\n\\nYou don't need an expensive news API initially.\\n\\nUse:\\n\\n### RSS feeds\\n\\nMany financial/news sites expose RSS feeds.\\n\\nYour pipeline:\\n\\n```text\\nRSS\\n ↓\\nPython feedparser\\n ↓\\nArticle text\\n ↓\\nDeduplication\\n ↓\\nNLP\\n ↓\\nDuckDB\\n```\\n\\nInstall:\\n\\n```bash\\npip install feedparser\\n```\\n\\nYou can also use Google News RSS for discovery.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 3. SEC/company filings — $0\\n\\nFor US-listed companies, SEC filings are publicly accessible.\\n\\nYou can download:\\n\\n- 10-K\\n- 10-Q\\n- 8-K\\n- 20-F\\n- company filings\\n\\nFor Stellantis specifically, also use its investor-relations/public filings.\\n\\nThis gives you **much better relationship evidence** than relying only on news articles.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 4. Database — don't use a cloud database\\n\\nUse **DuckDB**.\\n\\nIt's perfect for this project.\\n\\n```text\\ndata/\\n├── raw/\\n├── processed/\\n└── warehouse/\\n    └── market.duckdb\\n```\\n\\nInstall:\\n\\n```bash\\npip install duckdb\\n```\\n\\nYou can store:\\n\\n```text\\nprices\\ncompanies\\nevents\\nrelationships\\nnews\\nfilings\\nevent_returns\\ncorrelations\\n```\\n\\nNo AWS RDS bill.\\n\\nNo Snowflake bill.\\n\\nNo PostgreSQL server to maintain.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 5. Data lake — local filesystem\\n\\nYou don't actually need S3.\\n\\nUse:\\n\\n```text\\ndata/raw/\\ndata/processed/\\ndata/features/\\n```\\n\\nStore data as:\\n\\n```text\\nParquet\\nCSV\\nJSON\\n```\\n\\nThis also lets you demonstrate **data-lake architecture** without paying for cloud storage.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 6. ETL — Python\\n\\nYou already know Python/data engineering, so make your pipeline:\\n\\n```text\\nExtract\\n ↓\\nValidate\\n ↓\\nTransform\\n ↓\\nLoad\\n```\\n\\nFor example:\\n\\n```text\\nYahoo Finance\\n      ↓\\nraw_prices.parquet\\n      ↓\\nclean_prices.parquet\\n      ↓\\nDuckDB\\n```\\n\\nUse:\\n\\n- Pandas or Polars\\n- PyArrow\\n- DuckDB\\n\\nAll free.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 7. Statistical engine — $0\\n\\nEverything you need is open source:\\n\\n```bash\\npip install pandas numpy scipy statsmodels scikit-learn\\n```\\n\\nCalculate:\\n\\n- Pearson correlation\\n- Spearman correlation\\n- Rolling correlation\\n- Beta\\n- Volatility\\n- Abnormal returns\\n- Event studies\\n- Regression\\n- p-values\\n- confidence intervals\\n- lead/lag correlation\\n\\nYou don't need an external API for any of this.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 8. Knowledge graph — $0\\n\\nYou have two choices.\\n\\n### Easiest\\n\\nUse NetworkX:\\n\\n```bash\\npip install networkx\\n```\\n\\nStore:\\n\\n```text\\nSTLA\\n ├── supplier → Company A\\n ├── partner → Company B\\n ├── competitor → Company C\\n └── technology → Company D\\n```\\n\\n### More impressive\\n\\nRun Neo4j locally.\\n\\nNeo4j Community Edition can run locally, so:\\n\\n```text\\nDocker\\n ↓\\nNeo4j\\n ↓\\nKnowledge graph\\n```\\n\\nNo cloud bill.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 9. Vector database — $0\\n\\nYou've already worked with Chroma/FAISS.\\n\\nUse:\\n\\n### Chroma\\n\\nor\\n\\n### FAISS\\n\\nStore embeddings for:\\n\\n- News\\n- Filings\\n- Earnings calls\\n- Historical events\\n- Company relationships\\n\\nThen:\\n\\n```text\\nUser question\\n      ↓\\nEmbedding\\n      ↓\\nVector search\\n      ↓\\nRelevant documents\\n      ↓\\nLLM\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 10. LLM — run it locally\\n\\nThis is where you can save the most money.\\n\\nInstall:\\n\\n**Ollama**\\n\\nand run a local model.\\n\\nFor example:\\n\\n```text\\nQwen\\nLlama\\nMistral\\n```\\n\\nYour architecture becomes:\\n\\n```text\\nFastAPI\\n   ↓\\nOllama\\n   ↓\\nLocal LLM\\n```\\n\\nNo OpenAI API bill.\\n\\nGiven your **64 GB RAM + RTX A1000 6 GB**, you can comfortably experiment with smaller quantized models. Larger models will be slower and may need more aggressive quantization.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 11. Embeddings — also local\\n\\nUse Hugging Face sentence-transformer models.\\n\\nFor example:\\n\\n```bash\\npip install sentence-transformers\\n```\\n\\nThen run embeddings locally.\\n\\nYou don't need OpenAI embeddings.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 12. RAG — completely local\\n\\nYour RAG pipeline can be:\\n\\n```text\\nSEC filing\\n      ↓\\nChunk\\n      ↓\\nLocal embedding\\n      ↓\\nFAISS/Chroma\\n      ↓\\nRetriever\\n      ↓\\nOllama\\n      ↓\\nAnswer\\n```\\n\\nThat is a legitimate AI engineering project without API costs.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 13. Dashboard — $0\\n\\nUse **Streamlit**.\\n\\n```bash\\npip install streamlit\\n```\\n\\nYour dashboard can have:\\n\\n### Page 1 — Market Overview\\n\\n```text\\nSTLA\\nCurrent movement\\nVolatility\\nSector movement\\nMarket movement\\n```\\n\\n### Page 2 — Impact Network\\n\\nInteractive graph.\\n\\n### Page 3 — Event Analysis\\n\\n```text\\nEvent\\nSTLA reaction\\nSupplier reaction\\nCompetitor reaction\\nAbnormal returns\\n```\\n\\n### Page 4 — AI Investigation\\n\\nChat interface:\\n\\n\u003e \\\"Why did these companies move when STLA fell?\\\"\\n\\n### Page 5 — Historical Events\\n\\nSearch and compare previous events.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 14. Don't use Kubernetes initially\\n\\nThis is important.\\n\\nYou **can** containerize everything:\\n\\n```text\\ndocker-compose.yml\\n\\nservices:\\n  ingestion\\n  processor\\n  duckdb\\n  neo4j\\n  ollama\\n  api\\n  dashboard\\n```\\n\\nBut don't immediately build a Kubernetes cluster.\\n\\nFor a portfolio project:\\n\\n**Docker Compose → Kubernetes**\\n\\nis a much better progression.\\n\\nOnce the application works:\\n\\n```text\\nDocker Compose\\n       ↓\\nKubernetes\\n       ↓\\nOpenTelemetry\\n       ↓\\nPrometheus\\n       ↓\\nGrafana\\n```\\n\\nThat gives you a second phase specifically demonstrating your cloud/infrastructure skills.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 15. Free deployment\\n\\nYou don't actually need to deploy the entire system publicly.\\n\\nRun the heavy components locally.\\n\\nDeploy only the frontend/API if you want a public demo.\\n\\nPossible free/limited options include:\\n\\n- GitHub\\n- Hugging Face Spaces\\n- Streamlit Community Cloud\\n- Vercel\\n- Cloudflare\\n\\nBut **don't put your LLM + Neo4j + data pipeline on free hosting**. Keep those local.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 16. GitHub becomes part of the project\\n\\nStructure it professionally:\\n\\n```text\\nstellantis-market-intelligence/\\n│\\n├── ingestion/\\n│   ├── market_data.py\\n│   ├── news.py\\n│   └── filings.py\\n│\\n├── processing/\\n│   ├── cleaning.py\\n│   ├── events.py\\n│   └── features.py\\n│\\n├── analytics/\\n│   ├── correlation.py\\n│   ├── event_study.py\\n│   ├── beta.py\\n│   └── anomaly.py\\n│\\n├── knowledge_graph/\\n│   ├── entities.py\\n│   └── relationships.py\\n│\\n├── ai/\\n│   ├── embeddings.py\\n│   ├── rag.py\\n│   └── agent.py\\n│\\n├── api/\\n│   └── main.py\\n│\\n├── dashboard/\\n│   └── app.py\\n│\\n├── infrastructure/\\n│   ├── docker/\\n│   ├── kubernetes/\\n│   └── terraform/\\n│\\n├── data/\\n│   ├── raw/\\n│   └── processed/\\n│\\n├── tests/\\n│\\n├── docker-compose.yml\\n├── requirements.txt\\n└── README.md\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 17. Your actual ₹0 stack\\n\\n| Component | Tool |\\n|---|---|\\n| Market data | `yfinance` / public sources |\\n| News | RSS |\\n| Filings | SEC/public filings |\\n| Storage | Parquet |\\n| Database | DuckDB |\\n| Processing | Pandas/Polars |\\n| Statistics | SciPy/Statsmodels |\\n| ML | Scikit-learn |\\n| Knowledge graph | NetworkX → Neo4j |\\n| Vector DB | FAISS/Chroma |\\n| Embeddings | Sentence Transformers |\\n| LLM | Ollama |\\n| Backend | FastAPI |\\n| Frontend | React/Streamlit |\\n| Containers | Docker |\\n| Orchestration | Kubernetes |\\n| IaC | Terraform |\\n| Monitoring | OpenTelemetry + Prometheus + Grafana |\\n| CI/CD | GitHub Actions |\\n| Code hosting | GitHub |\\n\\n**Cost: ₹0.**\\n\\nThe only real constraint is **data quality and rate limits**, not compute cost.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## The smartest way to build it\\n\\nDon't try to build all 30 features immediately.\\n\\n### Phase 1 — MVP\\n\\n```text\\nSTLA\\n ↓\\nHistorical prices\\n ↓\\nDetect large movements\\n ↓\\nFind other stocks that moved\\n ↓\\nCorrelation + beta\\n ↓\\nDashboard\\n```\\n\\n### Phase 2 — Intelligence\\n\\n```text\\nNews\\n ↓\\nEvent classification\\n ↓\\nEvent study\\n ↓\\nCompany relationships\\n ↓\\nKnowledge graph\\n```\\n\\n### Phase 3 — AI\\n\\n```text\\nNews + filings\\n ↓\\nEmbeddings\\n ↓\\nRAG\\n ↓\\nLocal LLM\\n ↓\\nAI investigation agent\\n```\\n\\n### Phase 4 — Cloud/Infrastructure\\n\\n```text\\nDocker\\n ↓\\nKubernetes\\n ↓\\nFastAPI\\n ↓\\nOpenTelemetry\\n ↓\\nPrometheus/Grafana\\n ↓\\nCI/CD\\n```\\n\\nThat progression is particularly useful for your profile because the **same project demonstrates data engineering, AI/ML, backend development, cloud infrastructure, DevOps and observability** rather than looking like six unrelated toy projects.\",{},\"9e456947-f51d-4421-875d-519463f4a6b7\",{\"_299\":313,\"_301\":-5,\"_103\":474},1791058374.262,1791058391.380001,{\"_294\":295,\"_296\":472},{\"_126\":396,\"_309\":-5,\"_130\":397,\"_132\":397,\"_134\":43},[473],\"ok cost\",{},\"3c61a297-9fa5-455d-bdba-d1a1a6181745\",{\"_299\":300,\"_301\":-5,\"_103\":526},1791058339.664831,1791058353.313958,{\"_294\":295,\"_296\":524},{\"_109\":481,\"_111\":17,\"_112\":482,\"_114\":483,\"_116\":484,\"_118\":50,\"_119\":485,\"_121\":486,\"_123\":124,\"_125\":17,\"_126\":487,\"_128\":129,\"_130\":488,\"_132\":488,\"_133\":50,\"_49\":50,\"_134\":17,\"_135\":489,\"_137\":490},{\"_289\":290,\"_291\":523},[],[],[],{\"_139\":491,\"_141\":492,\"_143\":493,\"_145\":494,\"_147\":495,\"_149\":496,\"_151\":497,\"_153\":498,\"_155\":499,\"_157\":500,\"_159\":501,\"_161\":502,\"_163\":503,\"_165\":504,\"_167\":505,\"_169\":506},[],\"4e138394-0832-4975-b44b-14d114d119f4\",\"2fd76d56-025e-4fa5-b260-7570b2103213\",\"5cb1e6eb-73ea-4825-b1b9-ad744856e26a\",[],{\"_92\":522,\"_238\":43,\"_239\":43,\"_240\":-5},{\"_92\":521,\"_238\":43,\"_239\":43,\"_240\":-5},{\"_92\":520,\"_238\":43,\"_239\":43,\"_240\":-5},{\"_92\":519,\"_238\":43,\"_239\":43,\"_240\":-5},{\"_92\":518,\"_238\":43,\"_239\":43,\"_240\":-5},{\"_92\":517,\"_238\":43,\"_239\":43,\"_240\":-5},{\"_92\":516,\"_238\":43,\"_239\":43,\"_240\":-5},{\"_92\":515,\"_238\":43,\"_239\":43,\"_240\":-5},{\"_92\":514,\"_238\":43,\"_239\":43,\"_240\":-5},{\"_92\":513,\"_238\":43,\"_239\":43,\"_240\":-5},{\"_92\":512,\"_238\":43,\"_239\":43,\"_240\":-5},{\"_92\":511,\"_238\":43,\"_239\":43,\"_240\":-5},{\"_92\":510,\"_238\":43,\"_239\":43,\"_240\":-5},{\"_92\":509,\"_238\":43,\"_239\":43,\"_240\":-5},{\"_92\":508,\"_238\":43,\"_239\":43,\"_240\":-5},{\"_92\":507,\"_238\":43,\"_239\":43,\"_240\":-5},\"ch1uec\",\"qm1e70\",\"10gcrd\",\"u3ufel\",\"3l6ltm\",\"o5fq2u\",\"q960e4\",\"k8ugmh\",\"8ukvcz\",\"0qqsjx\",\"6675l7\",\"qhcaec\",\"mx99d5\",\"q0bj3b\",\"hawbse\",\"swrdac\",[293],[525],\"If you want to push this from a **good project** into something that looks like a serious internal **Stellantis market-intelligence platform**, I'd add features around **causal analysis, dependency mapping, simulation, anomaly detection, and autonomous investigation**.\\n\\n### 16. Event fingerprinting\\n\\nBuild a library of historical STLA events and their market fingerprints.\\n\\nFor every event:\\n\\n```text\\nEvent\\n├── Type: Production / Earnings / Recall / Partnership / Regulation\\n├── Severity\\n├── STLA abnormal return\\n├── Sector abnormal return\\n├── Supplier reaction\\n├── Competitor reaction\\n├── Commodity reaction\\n└── Recovery time\\n```\\n\\nThen when a new event occurs:\\n\\n\u003e “This event resembles 7 historical Stellantis events.”\\n\\nThat gives your system historical context automatically.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### 17. Similar-event search\\n\\nThis would work extremely well with embeddings/RAG.\\n\\nUser:\\n\\n\u003e “Find events similar to today's Stellantis announcement.”\\n\\nSystem searches historical events based on:\\n\\n- Text similarity\\n- Event type\\n- Business area\\n- Market reaction\\n- Companies involved\\n\\nThen:\\n\\n```text\\nCurrent event\\n     ↓\\nEmbedding\\n     ↓\\nVector DB\\n     ↓\\nTop 5 historical events\\n     ↓\\nCompare market reactions\\n```\\n\\nYou could use **FAISS/Chroma** here, which fits your existing AI work.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### 18. Causal graph\\n\\nTake the knowledge graph one step further.\\n\\nInstead of:\\n\\n```text\\nSTLA ─── Supplier A\\n```\\n\\nrepresent:\\n\\n```text\\nSTLA production ↓\\n       ↓\\nComponent orders ↓\\n       ↓\\nSupplier A revenue exposure\\n       ↓\\nSupplier A earnings expectations\\n       ↓\\nSupplier A stock reaction\\n```\\n\\nThe system distinguishes:\\n\\n**Observed relationship**\\n\\nfrom\\n\\n**hypothesized causal pathway**.\\n\\nThat distinction would make the project much more rigorous.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### 19. Shock propagation simulator\\n\\nGive the user a slider:\\n\\n\u003e **STLA production disruption: 5% → 50%**\\n\\nThen visualize the ecosystem exposure.\\n\\n```text\\n                    STLA\\n                     │\\n             Production -20%\\n                     │\\n        ┌────────────┼────────────┐\\n        ↓            ↓            ↓\\n   Supplier A    Supplier B   Logistics C\\n    Exposure       Exposure      Exposure\\n      HIGH          MEDIUM         LOW\\n```\\n\\nDon't convert that into predicted stock prices. Make it a **business exposure simulation**.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### 20. Supply-chain concentration score\\n\\nAsk:\\n\\n\u003e “How dependent is Stellantis on a particular company?”\\n\\nFor each supplier:\\n\\n```text\\nSupplier dependency\\n────────────────────────\\nRevenue exposure       72%\\nProduct criticality    HIGH\\nAlternative suppliers  LOW\\nGeographic diversity   MEDIUM\\nRelationship duration  8 years\\n```\\n\\nYou can also reverse it:\\n\\n\u003e “How dependent is Supplier A on Stellantis?”\\n\\nThat's potentially very useful for **supplier-risk intelligence**.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### 21. Second-order impact detection\\n\\nThis is where the project gets genuinely interesting.\\n\\nDon't stop at direct relationships.\\n\\n```text\\nSTLA\\n ↓\\nSupplier A\\n ↓\\nSupplier A's supplier B\\n ↓\\nCommodity producer C\\n```\\n\\nYour system identifies:\\n\\n\u003e **1st-order exposure**\\n\\nand\\n\\n\u003e **2nd/3rd-order exposure**\\n\\nThat is much more sophisticated than standard stock correlation.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### 22. Hidden relationship discovery\\n\\nUse NLP over:\\n\\n- Annual reports\\n- 10-K/20-F filings\\n- Earnings transcripts\\n- Investor presentations\\n- Press releases\\n\\nto discover relationships that aren't explicitly in your database.\\n\\nFor example:\\n\\n\u003e “Company X supplies component Y used in platform Z.”\\n\\nThe AI extracts:\\n\\n```text\\nCompany X\\n   ↓ supplies\\nComponent Y\\n   ↓ used in\\nStellantis Platform Z\\n```\\n\\nThen asks for evidence.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### 23. Relationship timeline\\n\\nRelationships aren't static.\\n\\nBuild:\\n\\n```text\\n2019 ─── Supplier relationship begins\\n2021 ─── New contract\\n2023 ─── Production expansion\\n2025 ─── New platform\\n2026 ─── Contract renewed\\n```\\n\\nThen correlate relationship changes with changes in stock sensitivity.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### 24. Market anomaly detector\\n\\nRun anomaly detection continuously.\\n\\nFor example:\\n\\n```text\\nNormal STLA volatility\\n        ↓\\nExpected movement: ±2%\\n\\nActual movement: -8%\\n        ↓\\nANOMALY\\n        ↓\\nInvestigate news / filings / options / sector\\n```\\n\\nThen automatically launch an investigation.\\n\\nThis can become an **agentic workflow**.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### 25. Autonomous investigation agent\\n\\nThis is probably the feature I'd prioritize most.\\n\\nInstead of the user manually asking questions:\\n\\n```text\\nSTLA moves \u003e threshold\\n          ↓\\n      AI Agent\\n          ↓\\n ┌────────┼─────────┐\\n ↓        ↓         ↓\\nNews   Market    Relationships\\n ↓        ↓         ↓\\n └────────┼─────────┘\\n          ↓\\n    Event hypothesis\\n          ↓\\n    Validate evidence\\n          ↓\\n  Identify affected firms\\n          ↓\\n   Generate report\\n```\\n\\nThe agent essentially says:\\n\\n\u003e **“Something unusual happened. Let me investigate why.”**\\n\\n---\\n\\n### 26. Market reaction replay\\n\\nCreate a historical **time machine**.\\n\\nSelect:\\n\\n\u003e September 2025 → Stellantis earnings event\\n\\nand replay:\\n\\n```text\\n09:30  STLA +0.2%\\n10:00  STLA -1.4%\\n10:30  Supplier A -2.1%\\n11:00  Competitor B +1.3%\\n12:00  STLA -4.2%\\n...\\n```\\n\\nThen visualize how the event propagated through the ecosystem.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### 27. Recovery analysis\\n\\nDon't only study the initial reaction.\\n\\nMeasure:\\n\\n```text\\nEvent\\n ↓\\nDay 0: -8%\\nDay 1: -3%\\nDay 3: +2%\\nDay 5: +5%\\nDay 20: +11%\\n```\\n\\nThen classify the historical response as:\\n\\n- Temporary shock\\n- Persistent repricing\\n- Gradual recovery\\n- Delayed reaction\\n\\nThis can reveal very different types of events.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### 28. Cross-asset analysis\\n\\nGo beyond equities.\\n\\nConnect STLA events with:\\n\\n- Commodities\\n- FX\\n- Interest rates\\n- ETFs\\n- Automotive indices\\n- Credit spreads\\n- Options/implied volatility\\n\\nFor example:\\n\\n```text\\nEUR/USD\\n   ↓\\nEuropean manufacturing\\n   ↓\\nSTLA\\n   ↓\\nSuppliers\\n```\\n\\nThis gives you a much broader macro-to-company analysis layer.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### 29. Options-market anomaly layer\\n\\nIf data availability permits, monitor:\\n\\n- Implied volatility\\n- Put/call activity\\n- Open interest\\n- Volume anomalies\\n- IV changes around events\\n\\nThen your system can identify:\\n\\n\u003e **“Equity movement was accompanied by an unusual options-market change.”**\\n\\nAgain, this is **analysis**, not a trading recommendation.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### 30. Natural-language graph querying\\n\\nThis could look extremely good in a demo.\\n\\nUser types:\\n\\n\u003e **“Show all companies within two degrees of Stellantis that have significant historical sensitivity to production-related events.”**\\n\\nThe AI converts that into graph + SQL queries.\\n\\nOr:\\n\\n\u003e **“Which battery companies are connected to Stellantis through documented relationships?”**\\n\\nOr:\\n\\n\u003e **“Show companies whose relationship with Stellantis strengthened after 2024.”**\\n\\nThat's a proper **AI data-agent use case**.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 31. Build an \\\"Impact Score\\\" — but make it analytical\\n\\nRather than ranking stocks as investments, calculate an **ecosystem exposure score** based on measurable components:\\n\\n```text\\nImpact Exposure\\n───────────────\\nBusiness relationship     30%\\nHistorical sensitivity     25%\\nRevenue exposure           20%\\nEvent relevance            15%\\nSupply-chain criticality   10%\\n```\\n\\nCall it something like:\\n\\n\u003e **STLA Ecosystem Exposure Index**\\n\\nNot:\\n\\n\u003e “Best stock to buy.”\\n\\nThat keeps the system focused on **corporate intelligence**.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 32. What I would ultimately build\\n\\nYour final product could look like:\\n\\n```text\\n┌──────────────────────────────────────────────┐\\n│        STELLANTIS MARKET INTELLIGENCE        │\\n├──────────────────────────────────────────────┤\\n│ STLA  -4.82%       ⚠ EVENT DETECTED          │\\n│                                              │\\n│ EVENT: Production / Strategic Announcement  │\\n├──────────────────────────────────────────────┤\\n│              IMPACT NETWORK                  │\\n│                                              │\\n│       Supplier A ──┐                         │\\n│                    ↓                         │\\n│              ┌─────────┐                     │\\n│ Competitor ─→│   STLA  │←── Battery Co.      │\\n│              └─────────┘                     │\\n│                    ↑                         │\\n│              Semiconductor                  │\\n├──────────────────────────────────────────────┤\\n│ AFFECTED COMPANIES                           │\\n│                                              │\\n│ Company       Exposure    Reaction    Evidence│\\n│ Supplier A      HIGH       -5.2%      ✓      │\\n│ Supplier B      MEDIUM     -2.1%      ✓      │\\n│ Competitor C    MEDIUM     +1.8%      ✓      │\\n├──────────────────────────────────────────────┤\\n│ AI INVESTIGATION                             │\\n│                                              │\\n│ \\\"The strongest documented relationship is   │\\n│ between STLA and Supplier A...\\\"             │\\n│                                              │\\n│ [Evidence] [Historical Events] [Filings]    │\\n└──────────────────────────────────────────────┘\\n```\\n\\n### The killer feature\\n\\nI'd make the homepage have one question:\\n\\n\u003e **“Something happened to Stellantis. What else should I investigate?”**\\n\\nThen the entire platform autonomously investigates:\\n\\n**event → companies → relationships → historical precedent → market reaction → supply-chain exposure → evidence → explanation**\\n\\nThat is a much stronger portfolio project than simply **“which stocks correlate with STLA?”** It also gives you concrete opportunities to demonstrate **Python, statistics, ML/NLP, vector databases, knowledge graphs, APIs, cloud architecture, Kubernetes, observability, and an AI agent** in one coherent
